The Senate shall also choose its other officers and in the absence of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> and its president pro tempore may, from time to time, as occasion may require, appoint </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of its members to preside. The House of Representatives at such first annual session shall choose </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of its members speaker and also choose its other officers, and in the absence of the speaker may from time to time, as occasion may require, appoint </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of its members to preside.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 31. Amend § 8, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">8</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">8.</span><span> Each House as judge of elections and qualifications of its members; quorum; adjournments; compelling attendance.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 8.</span><span> Each House shall be the judge of the elections, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">returns</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">returns, </span><span>and qualifications of its own members; and a majority of all the members elected to each House shall constitute a quorum to do business; but a smaller number may adjourn from day to day, and shall have power to compel the attendance of absent members, in such manner and under such penalties, as shall be deemed expedient.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 32. Amend § 9, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 9. Rules; punishment and expulsion of members; scope of powers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 9.</span><span> Each House may determine the rules of its proceedings, punish any of its members for disorderly behavior, and with the concurrence of two-thirds of all the members elected thereto expel a member, and shall have all other powers necessary for a branch of the Legislature of a free and independent State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 33. Amend § 10, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 10. Journals; publication; entry of yeas and nays; passage of bills and resolutions.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 10.</span><span> Each House shall keep a journal of its proceedings, and publish the same immediately after every session, except such parts as may require secrecy. The names of the members voting for and against any bill or joint resolution, except in relation to adjournment, shall on the final vote be entered on the journal; and the yeas and nays of the members on any question shall, at the desire of any member, be entered on the journal. No bill or joint resolution, except in relation to adjournment, shall pass either House unless the final vote shall have been taken by yeas and nays, nor without the concurrence of a majority of all the members elected to each House.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 34. Amend § 11, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 11. Accessibility to each House and Committees of the Whole.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 11.</span><span> The doors of each House, and of Committees of the Whole, shall be open unless when the business is such as ought to be kept secret.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 35. Amend § 12, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 12. Consent of each House to adjournment.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 12.</span><span> Neither House shall, without the consent of the other, adjourn for more than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3</span><span> days, nor to any other place than that in which the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2</span><span> Houses shall be sitting.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 36. Amend § 13, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 13. Immunity from arrest and questioning of speeches.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 13.</span><span> The Senators and Representatives shall, in all cases, except treason, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">felony</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">felony,</span><span> or breach of the peace, be privileged from arrest during their attendance at the session of their respective Houses, and in going to and returning from the same; and for any speech or debate in either House they shall not be questioned in any other place.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 37. Amend § 14, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 14. </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Holding dual office or</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Appointment to certain civil offices; holding dual office;</span><span> having interest in army or navy contract.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 14.</span><span> No Senator or Representative shall, during the time for which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> that person</span><span> shall have been elected, be appointed to any civil office under this State which shall have been created, or the emoluments of which shall have been increased during such time. No member of Congress, nor any person holding any office under this State, or the United States, except officers usually appointed by the courts of justice respectively, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">attorneys-at-law</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">attorneys-at-law, </span><span>and officers of the militia, holding no disqualifying office, shall during</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> continuance in Congress or in office be a Senator or Representative; nor shall any person while concerned in any army or navy contract be a Senator or Representative.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 38. Amend § 15, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 15. Compensation,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> expenses</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> expenses,</span><span> and allowances of members.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 15.</span><span> The President of the Senate and members of the General Assembly shall receive an annual salary and an annual expense allowance for transportation and such other necessary and proper purposes as the General Assembly shall by law provide. Funds appropriated hereunder shall be paid out of the Treasury of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 39. Amend § 16, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 16. Restriction of bills and resolutions to one subject; expression in title; exception.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 16.</span><span> No bill or joint resolution, except bills appropriating money for public purposes, shall embrace more than one subject, which shall be expressed in its title.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 40. Amend § 17, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 17. Lotteries and other gambling.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a) </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17. </span><span>All forms of gambling are prohibited in this State except the following:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(a)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> Lotteries under State control for the purpose of raising</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> funds,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> funds.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(b)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> Lotteries (other than slot machines, roulette,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> craps</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> craps,</span><span> and baccarat games) provided that each is sponsored and conducted under the limitations of Section 17B </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">of this Article</span><span> by companies, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">organizations</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> organizations,</span><span> or societies which have been in existence for at least 2 years; provided, however, that no person who shall not have attained the age of 18 years shall participate in any lottery (where money is the prize) otherwise authorized by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the article,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(c)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> Wagering or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> betting</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> betting,</span><span> by the use of pari-mutuel machines or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> totalizators</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> totalizators,</span><span> on horse races conducted at racetracks within or without</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, provided that such wagering or betting may be conducted only either:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 36pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(1)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">a.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">within</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Within</span><span> the enclosure of any racetrack licensed under the laws of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State to conduct a race</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> meeting, or</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> meeting.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 36pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(2)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">b.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">within</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Within</span><span> the enclosure of any racetrack licensed under the laws of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State to receive and accept wagers or bets on electronically televised simulcasts of horse races.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(d)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(4)</span><span> Bingo games as conducted under the limitations of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17A.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section 17A of this Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> The General Assembly shall enforce this Section by appropriate legislation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 41. Amend § 17A, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 17A. Bingo games; organizations authorized to conduct; submission to referendum; districts; regulation; penalties.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17A.</span><span> The game of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Bingo</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bingo</span><span> shall be lawful when sponsored and conducted by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Volunteer Fire Companies, Veterans' Organizations, Religious or Charitable Organizations, or by Fraternal Societies</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> volunteer fire companies, veterans organizations, religious or charitable organizations, or by fraternal societies</span><span> provided the net receipts or profits arising from the conducting or operating of such</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Bingo</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bingo</span><span> games by the aforementioned</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Companies, Organizations, or Societies</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> companies, organizations, or societies</span><span> are used solely for the promotion or achievement of the purposes of such</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Companies, Organizations, or Societies,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> companies, organizations, or societies,</span><span> and provided further that the aforementioned</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Companies, Organizations or Societies</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> companies, organizations, or societies</span><span> are operated in a manner so as to come within the provisions of Section 170 of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> U. S.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> U.S.</span><span> Revenue Code and Regulations promulgated thereunder by the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> U. S.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> U.S.</span><span> Secretary of the Treasury.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">1.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)(1)</span><span> The General Assembly shall provide by law for the submission to the vote of the qualified electors of the several districts of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, or any of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">them, mentioned in subparagraph 2 of Section 17A of this article</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the districts, mentioned in paragraph (b)(2) of this Section </span><span>at the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> General Election</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> general election</span><span> held in 1958, the question whether the playing of the game of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> "Bingo"</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bingo</span><span> shall be licensed or prohibited within the limits thereof; and in every district in which there is a majority against license, no organization, mentioned in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17A,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">this Section,</span><span> shall thereafter sponsor or permit the playing of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> "Bingo",</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bingo</span><span> within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> district, until at a subsequent submission of such question a majority of votes shall be cast in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> district for license. Whenever a majority of all the members elected to each House of the General Assembly by the qualified electors in any</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> district</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> district,</span><span> named in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">subparagraph 2 of Section 17A of this Article</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> paragraph (b)(2) of this Section,</span><span> shall request the submission of the question of license or no license to a vote of the qualified electors in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> district, the General Assembly shall provide for the submission of such question to the qualified electors in such district at the next general election thereafter.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">2.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> Under </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the provisions of</span><span> this Article, Sussex County shall comprise </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>district, Kent County shall comprise </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>district, the City of Wilmington, as its corporate limits now are or may hereafter be extended, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>district, and the remaining part of New Castle County, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>district.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">3.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> The General Assembly shall provide necessary laws to carry out and enforce the provisions of this Article, enact laws governing the game of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> "Bingo"</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bingo</span><span> under the limitations of this Article, and may provide such penalties as may be necessary to enforce same.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 42. Amend § 17B, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 17B. Lotteries not under State control; organizations authorized to conduct; submission to referendum; districts; regulation; penalties.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17B. </span><span>Lotteries not under State control shall be lawful when sponsored and conducted by volunteer fire companies, veterans organizations, religious or charitable organizations, or by fraternal societies provided that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> company,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> organization</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> organization,</span><span> or society has been in existence a minimum of 2 years and provided the net receipts or profits arising from the conducting or operating of such lotteries by the aforementioned companies,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> organizations</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> organizations,</span><span> or societies are used solely for the promotion or achievement of the purposes of such companies,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> organizations</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> organizations,</span><span> or societies, and provided further that the aforementioned companies,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> organizations</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> organizations,</span><span> or societies are operated in a manner so as to come within § 170 of the United States Revenue Code and regulations promulgated thereunder by the United States Secretary of the Treasury.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">1.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)(1)</span><span> The General Assembly shall provide by law for the submission to the vote of the qualified electors of the several districts of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, or any of them, mentioned in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">paragraph 2 of this section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">paragraph (b)(2) of this Section </span><span>at the general election held in 1984, the question whether the playing of lotteries not under State control shall be licensed or prohibited within the limits thereof; and in every district in which there is a majority against license, no organization, mentioned in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section, </span><span>shall thereafter sponsor or permit lotteries not under State control, within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the </span><span>district, until at a subsequent submission of such question a majority of votes shall be cast in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the </span><span>district for license. Whenever a majority of all the members elected to each House of the General Assembly by the qualified electors in any district named in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">paragraph 2 of this section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">paragraph (b)(2) of this Section </span><span>shall request the submission of the question of license or no license to a vote of the qualified electors in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> district, the General Assembly shall provide for the submission of such question to the qualified electors in such district at the next general election thereafter.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">2.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> Under this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">article,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article, </span><span>Sussex County shall comprise 1 district, Kent County shall comprise 1 district, the City of Wilmington, as its corporate limits now are or may hereafter be extended, 1 district, and the remaining part of New Castle County, 1 district.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">3.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> The General Assembly shall enact comprehensive legislation providing for licensing for all organizations conducting and regulating the conduct of lotteries under this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section</span><span> and may provide such penalties as may be necessary to enforce such legislation. </span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 43. Amend § 18, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 18. Divorce or alimony.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 18. </span><span>No divorce shall be granted, nor alimony allowed, except by the judgment of a court, as shall be prescribed by general and uniform law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 44. Amend § 19, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 19. Local or special laws relating to fences, livestock, ditches, school districts, and roads, highways, streets, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">etc.</span><span></span><span></span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">lanes, or alleys.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 19. </span><span>The General Assembly shall not pass any local or special law relating to fences; the straying of livestock; ditches; the creation or changing the boundaries of school districts; or the laying out, opening, alteration,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> maintenance</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> maintenance,</span><span> or vacation, in whole or in part of any road, highway, street,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> lane</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> lane,</span><span> or alley; provided, however, that the General Assembly may by a vote of two-thirds of all the members elected to each House pass laws relating to the laying out, opening,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> alteration</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> alteration,</span><span> or maintenance of any road or highway which forms a continuous road or highway extending through at least a portion of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3 </span><span>counties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> No road,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> highway</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> highway,</span><span> or street, intended to be dedicated to public use and maintained at public expense, shall be constructed except in conformance with standards adopted by the agency charged with construction, reconstruction or maintenance of such road, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">highway</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">highway, </span><span>or street. Any road or street constructed solely for private use shall only be maintained at State expense after it has been constructed or reconstructed according to the standards established by the agency charged with the duty of maintaining such roads or streets.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 45. Amend § 20, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 20. Disclosure of personal or private interest of legislator in any pending measure.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 20. </span><span>Any member of the General Assembly who has a personal or private interest in any measure or bill pending in the General Assembly shall disclose the fact to the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">House of which he or she is a member</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> member’s House</span><span> and shall not vote thereon.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 46. Amend § 21, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 21. Conviction of crime as ban to public office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 21. </span><span>No person who shall be convicted of embezzlement of the public money, bribery,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> perjury</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> perjury,</span><span> or other infamous crime, shall be eligible to a seat in either House of the General Assembly, or capable of holding any office of trust,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> honor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> honor,</span><span> or profit under this State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 47. Amend § 22, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 22. Bribery of executive, judicial or legislative officers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 22. </span><span>Every person who shall give,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> offer</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> offer,</span><span> or promise, directly or indirectly, any money, testimonial, privilege,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> personal advantage</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> personal advantage,</span><span> or thing of value to any executive or judicial officer of this State or to any member of either House of the General Assembly for the purpose of influencing</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her in</span><span> the performance of any</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> of his or her</span><span> official or public duties shall be deemed guilty of bribery, and shall be punished in such manner as shall be provided by law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 48. Amend § 23, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 23. Statutes as public laws unless otherwise declared.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 23. </span><span>Every statute shall be a public law unless otherwise declared in the statute itself.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 49. Amend § 24, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 24. Settlement of accounts of State Treasurer; ineligibility for legislative office until settlement.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>[Repealed.]</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 50. Amend § 25, Article II of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 25. Laws permitting zoning ordinances and use of land.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 25. </span><span>The General Assembly may enact laws under which municipalities and the County of Sussex and the County of Kent and the County of New Castle may adopt zoning ordinances,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> laws</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> laws,</span><span> or rules limiting and restricting to specified districts and regulating therein buildings and structures according to their construction and the nature and extent of their use, as well as the use to be made of land in such districts for other than agricultural purposes; and the exercise of such authority shall be deemed to be within the police power of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 51. Amend § 1, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 1. Governor to be supreme executive.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 1. </span><span>The supreme executive powers of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State shall be vested in a Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 52. Amend § 2, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 2. Election of Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 2. </span><span>The Governor shall be chosen by the qualified electors of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, once in every </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">four</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">4 </span><span>years, at the general election.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 53. Amend § 3, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 3. Election returns, publications; election by General Assembly.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 3.</span><span> The returns of every election for Governor shall be sealed up and immediately transmitted to the President of the Senate, or in case of a vacancy in the office of President of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Senate, or his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Senate or </span><span>absence from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the State</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this State,</span><span> to the Secretary of State, who shall keep the same until a President of the Senate shall be </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">chosen,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">chosen or returns to this State, </span><span>to whom they shall be immediately transmitted after</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her election,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the President of the Senate’s election or return to this State, </span><span>who shall open and publish the same in the presence of the members of both Houses of the General Assembly. Duplicates of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> returns shall also be immediately lodged with the Prothonotary of each county. The person having the highest number of votes shall be Governor; but if </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2 </span><span>or more shall be equal in the highest number of votes, the members of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2</span><span> Houses shall, by joint ballot, choose </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of them to be Governor; and if, upon such ballot, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2 </span><span>or more of them shall still be equal and highest in votes, the President of the Senate shall have the casting vote.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 54. Amend § 4, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 4. Contested elections of Governor or </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Lieutenant-Governor.</span><span></span><span></span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 4.</span><span> Contested elections of the Governor or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Lieutenant Governor</span><span> shall be determined by a joint committee, consisting of one-third of all the members elected to each House of the General Assembly, to be selected by ballot of the Houses respectively. Every member of the committee shall take an oath or affirmation that in determining the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> election</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the member</span><span> will faithfully discharge the trust reposed in</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her;</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the member;</span><span> and the committee shall always sit with open doors.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> The Chief Justice, or, in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> absence or disability, the Chancellor shall preside at the trial of any contested election of Governor or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> and shall decide questions regarding the admissibility of evidence, and shall, upon request of the committee, pronounce</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> an</span><span> opinion upon other questions of law involved in the trial.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 55. Amend § 5, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 5. Term of office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 5. </span><span>The Governor shall hold</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office during </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">four</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">4</span><span> years from the third Tuesday in January next ensuing</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor’s</span><span> election; and shall not be elected a third time to</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> that</span><span> office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 56. Amend § 6, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 6. Qualifications.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 6. </span><span>The Governor shall be at least </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">thirty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">30</span><span> years of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> age,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> age</span><span> and have been a citizen and inhabitant of the United States </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">twelve</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">12</span><span> years next before the day of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> election, and the last </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">six</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">6</span><span> years of that term an inhabitant of this State, unless</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she shall have been</span><span> absent on public business of the United States or of this State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 57. Amend § 7, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 7. Compensation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 7. </span><span>The Governor shall, at stated times, receive for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> services an adequate salary to be fixed by law, which shall be neither increased nor diminished during the period for which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall have been elected.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 58. Amend § 8, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 8. Governor as commander-in-chief of state armed forces.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 8. He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall be commander-in-chief of the army and navy of this State, and of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> </span><span>militia, except when they shall be called into the service of the United States.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 59. Amend § 9, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 9. Appointing power; recess appointments; confirmation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 9. He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall have power, unless herein otherwise provided, to appoint, by and with the consent of a majority of all the members elected to the Senate, such officers as </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> is or may be authorized by this Constitution or by law to appoint.</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall have power to fill all vacancies that may happen during the recess of the Senate, in offices to which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> may appoint, except in the offices of Chancellor, Chief Justice and Judges, by granting Commissions which shall expire at the end of the next session of the Senate.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall have power to fill all vacancies that may happen in elective offices, except in the offices of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> and members of the General Assembly, by granting Commissions which shall expire when their successors shall be duly qualified.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> In case of vacancy in an elective office, except as aforesaid, a person shall be chosen to</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> office for the full term at the next general election, unless the vacancy shall happen within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2 </span><span>months next before such election, in which case the election for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> office shall be held at the second succeeding general election.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> Unless herein otherwise provided, confirmation by the Senate of officers appointed by the Governor shall be required only where the salary,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> fees</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> fees,</span><span> and emoluments of office shall exceed the sum of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">fifteen hundred dollars</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">$1,500 </span><span>annually.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 60. Amend § 10, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 10. Secretary of State; appointment, term,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> duties</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> duties,</span><span> and compensation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 10.</span><span> The Governor shall appoint, by and with the consent of a majority of all the members elected to the Senate, a Secretary of State, who shall hold office during the pleasure of the Governor.</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Secretary of State</span><span> shall keep a fair register of all the official acts and proceedings of the Governor, and shall, when required by either House of the General Assembly lay the same, and all papers,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> minutes</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> minutes,</span><span> and vouchers, relative thereto, before such House, and shall perform such other duties as shall be enjoined upon</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Secretary of State</span><span> by law.</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Secretary of State</span><span> shall have a compensation for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> service to be fixed by law. The Secretary of State shall become a bona fide resident of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">six</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">6 </span><span>months after</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> appointment; provided, however, that upon good cause shown, the Governor may grant an additional extension of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">six</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">6 </span><span>months. After becoming a resident of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, the Secretary</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> of State</span><span> shall continuously be a resident of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> as long as he or she retains</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> while in</span><span> office. Failure to obtain or retain such residency shall be an automatic resignation from office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 61. Amend § 11, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 11. County officers; qualifications; members of Congress, federal</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> employees</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> employees,</span><span> and other officers holding dual office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 11. </span><span>No person shall be elected or appointed to an office within a county who shall not have a right to vote for a Representative in the General</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Assembly,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Assembly</span><span> and have been a resident therein </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1</span><span> year next before</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person’s</span><span> election or appointment, nor hold the office longer than</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person</span><span> continues to reside in the county, unless herein otherwise provided.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> No member of Congress, nor any person holding or exercising any office under the United States, except officers usually appointed by the courts of justice respectively and attorneys-at-law, shall at the same time hold or exercise any office of profit under this State, unless herein otherwise provided.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> No person shall hold more than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of the following offices at the same </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">time, to-wit:</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> time:</span><span> Secretary of State,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Attorney-General,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Attorney General,</span><span> Insurance Commissioner, State Treasurer, Auditor of Accounts, Prothonotary, Clerk of the Peace, Register of Wills, Recorder, or Sheriff.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 62. Amend § 12, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 12. Commissions.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 12.</span><span> All Commissions shall be in the name of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the State,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this State</span><span> and shall be sealed with the great seal and signed by the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 63. Amend § 13, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 13. Removal of officers by Governor; procedure.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 13. </span><span>The Governor may for any reasonable cause remove any officer, except the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> and members of the General Assembly, upon the address of two-thirds of all the members elected to each House of the General Assembly. Whenever the General Assembly shall so address the Governor, the cause of removal shall be entered on the journals of each House. The person against whom the General Assembly may be about to proceed shall receive notice thereof, accompanied with the cause alleged for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person’s</span><span> removal, at least </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">ten</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">10</span><span> days before the day on which either House of the General Assembly shall act thereon.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 64. Amend § 14, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 14. Reports from executive departments.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 14. </span><span>The Governor may require information in writing from the officers in the executive department, upon any subject relating to the duties of their respective offices.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 65. Amend § 15, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 15. Messages to General Assembly.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 15. He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall, from time to time, give to the General Assembly information of affairs concerning</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State and recommend to its consideration such measures as</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall judge expedient.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 66. Amend § 16, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 16. Special sessions of General Assembly; adjournment; special session of Senate.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 16. He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> may on extraordinary occasions convene the General Assembly by proclamation; and in case of disagreement between the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> Houses </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">of the General Assembly </span><span>with respect to the time of adjournment, adjourn them to such time as</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall think proper, not exceeding </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3</span><span> months.</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall have power to convene the Senate in extraordinary session by proclamation, for the transaction of executive business.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 67. Amend § 17, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 17. Execution of laws.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17. He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Governor</span><span> shall take care that the laws be faithfully executed.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 68. Amend § 18, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> 18</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> 18.</span><span> Approval or veto of bills, orders,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> resolutions</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> resolutions,</span><span> or votes; repassage over veto.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 18.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(a) Every bill which shall have passed both Houses of the General Assembly shall, before it becomes law, be presented to the Governor; if</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> approves,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall sign it; but if</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall not approve,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall return </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">it with his or her objections</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">it, with objections, </span><span>to the House in which it shall have originated, which House shall enter the objections at large on the journal and proceed to reconsider it. If, after such reconsideration, three-fifths of all the members elected to that House shall agree to pass the bill, it shall be sent together with the objections to the other House, by which it shall likewise be reconsidered, and if approved by three-fifths of all the members elected to that House, it shall become a law; but in neither House shall the vote be taken on the day on which the bill shall be returned to it. In all such cases the votes of both Houses shall be determined by yeas and nays, and the names of the members voting for and against the bill shall be entered on the journal of each House respectively. If any bill shall not be returned by the Governor within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">ten</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">10</span><span> days, Sundays excepted, after it shall have been presented to</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor,</span><span> the same shall be a law in like manner as if</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> had signed it, unless the General Assembly shall, by final adjournment, prevent its return, in which case it shall not become a law without the approval of the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(b) For purposes of return of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Bills</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bills</span><span> not approved by the Governor the General Assembly shall be considered to be continuously in Session until final adjournment and the Clerk of the House of Representatives and the Secretary of the Senate shall be deemed proper recipients of such returned bills during recess or adjournment of the General Assembly other than final adjournment.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(c) No bill shall become a law after the final adjournment of the General Assembly, unless approved by the Governor within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">thirty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">30 </span><span>days after such adjournment. The Governor shall have power to disapprove of any item or items of any bill making appropriations of money, embracing distinct items, and the part or parts of the bill approved shall be the law, and the item or items of appropriation disapproved shall be void, unless repassed according to the rules and limitations prescribed for the passage of other bills, over the Executive veto.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(d) Every order and resolution to which the concurrence of both Houses of the General Assembly may be necessary, except on a question of adjournment and those matters dealing solely with the internal or administrative affairs of the General Assembly, shall be presented to the Governor, and before the same shall take effect be approved by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor,</span><span> or being disapproved by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor,</span><span> shall be repassed by three-fifths of all the members elected to each House of the General Assembly, according to the rules and limitations prescribed in the case of a bill.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 69. Amend § 19, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 19. </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Lieutenant-Governor;</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Lieutenant Governor;</span><span> election,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> term</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> term,</span><span> and qualifications; President of the Senate; compensation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 19. A Lieutenant-Governor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> A Lieutenant Governor</span><span> shall be chosen at the same time, in the same manner, for the same term, and subject to the same provisions as the Governor;</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Lieutenant Governor</span><span> shall possess the same qualifications of eligibility for office as the Governor;</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Lieutenant Governor</span><span> shall be President of the Senate, but shall have no vote unless the Senate be equally divided.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
