<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> The</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor,</span><span> for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> services as President of the Senate, shall receive the same compensation as the Speaker of the House of Representatives; the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> services as a member of the Board of Pardons and for all other duties of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> office which may be provided by law, shall receive such compensation as shall be fixed by the General Assembly.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 70. Amend § 20, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 20. Vacancy in offices of both Governor and</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor;</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor;</span><span> officers eligible to act; disability of Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 20.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(a)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> In case the person elected Governor shall die or become disqualified before the commencement of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person elected Governor’s</span><span> term of office, or shall refuse to take the same, or in case of the removal of the Governor from office, or of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor’s</span><span> death, resignation, or inability to discharge the powers and duties of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> office, the same shall devolve on the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor;</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor;</span><span> and in case of removal, death, resignation, or inability of both the Governor and</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor,</span><span> the Secretary of State, or if there be none, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Secretary of State’s</span><span> removal, death, resignation, or inability, then the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Attorney-General,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Attorney General,</span><span> or if there be none, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Attorney General’s</span><span> removal, death, resignation, or inability, then the President pro tempore of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Senate</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Senate,</span><span> or if there be none, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the President pro tempore’s</span><span> removal, death, resignation, or inability, then the Speaker of the House of Representatives shall act as Governor until the disability of the Governor or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Lieutenant-Governor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor</span><span> is removed, or a Governor shall be duly elected and qualified.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">The foregoing provisions of this</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Paragraph (a)(1) of this Section </span><span>shall apply only to such persons as are eligible to the office of Governor under this Constitution at the time the powers and duties of the office of Governor shall devolve upon them respectively.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> Whenever the powers and duties of the office of Governor shall devolve upon the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Lieutenant-Governor,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Lieutenant Governor,</span><span> Secretary of State, or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Attorney-General,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Attorney General, their</span><span> office shall become vacant; and whenever the powers and duties of the office of Governor shall devolve upon the President pro tempore of the Senate, or the Speaker of the House of Representatives,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">their</span><span> seat as a member of the General Assembly shall become vacant; and any such vacancy shall be filled as directed by this Constitution; provided, however, that such vacancy shall not be created in case </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">either of the said persons</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the Lieutenant Governor, Secretary of State, Attorney General, President pro tempore of the Senate, or the Speaker of the House of Representatives </span><span>shall be acting as Governor during a temporary disability of the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(b) Whenever the Governor transmits to the President pro tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor’s</span><span> written declaration that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> is unable to discharge the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office, and until</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> transmits to them a written declaration to the contrary, such powers and duties shall be discharged by the Lieutenant Governor as Acting Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(c)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> Whenever the Chief Justice of the Delaware Supreme Court, the President of the Medical Society of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Delaware</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Delaware,</span><span> and the Director of the Division of Substance Abuse and Mental Health, acting unanimously, transmit to the President pro tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives, their written declaration that the Governor is unable to discharge the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office because of mental or physical disability, the Lieutenant Governor shall immediately assume the powers and duties of the office as Acting Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> Thereafter, when the Governor transmits to the President pro tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor’s</span><span> written declaration that no disability exists,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> shall resume the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office unless the Chief Justice of the Supreme Court of Delaware, the President of the Medical Society of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Delaware</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Delaware,</span><span> and the Director of the Division of Substance Abuse and Mental Health, acting unanimously, transmit within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">five</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">5</span><span> days to the President pro tempore of the Senate and the Speaker of the House of Representatives their written declaration that the Governor is unable to discharge the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office because of mental or physical disability. Thereupon the General Assembly shall decide the issue, assembling within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">seventy-two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">72 </span><span>hours for that purpose if not then in session. If the General Assembly within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">ten</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">10</span><span> days after receipt of the latter written declaration determines by two-thirds vote of all the members elected to each house that the Governor is unable to discharge the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office because of mental or physical disability, the Lieutenant Governor shall continue to discharge same as Acting Governor; otherwise, the Governor shall resume the powers and duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 71. Amend § 21, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 21. Election and term of office for certain state officers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 21.</span><span> The terms of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the Office</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">office </span><span>of the Attorney General, the Insurance Commissioner, the Auditor of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Accounts</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Accounts,</span><span> and the State Treasurer shall be 4 years. These officers shall be chosen by the qualified electors of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State at general</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> elections,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> elections</span><span> and be commissioned by the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 72. Amend § 22, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 22. Election and term of office of certain county officers; commission.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 22. </span><span>The terms of office of Clerks of the Peace, Registers of Wills, Recorders, and Sheriffs shall be 4 years. These officers shall be chosen by the qualified electors of the respective counties at general</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> elections,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> elections</span><span> and be commissioned by the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 73. Amend § 23, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 23. Place of office of certain county officers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 23. </span><span>Prothonotaries, Clerks of the Peace, Registers of Wills,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Recorders</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Recorders,</span><span> and Sheriffs shall keep their offices in the town or place in each county in which the Superior Court is usually held.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 74. Amend § 24, Article III of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 24. Date of commencement of terms of certain State and County elective officers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>The terms of office of all State and County officers made elective by this Constitution begin on the first Tuesday in January next after their election, unless otherwise provided in this Constitution.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 75. Amend § 1, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 1. Creation of courts.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 1.</span><span> The judicial power of this State shall be vested in a Supreme Court, a Superior Court, a Court of Chancery, a Family Court, a Court of Common Pleas, a Register's Court, Justices of the Peace, and such other courts as the General Assembly, with the concurrence of two-thirds of all the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Members</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">members</span><span> elected to each House, shall have by law established </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">prior to the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">before May 14, 1951, </span><span>or shall from time to time by law establish after such time.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 76. Amend § 2, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 2. Justices of Supreme Court and other State Judges; qualifications; residence; precedence.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 2. </span><span>There shall be </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">five</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">5</span><span> Justices of the Supreme Court who shall be citizens of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State and learned in the law. One of them shall be the Chief Justice who shall be designated as such by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> appointment and who when present shall preside at all sittings of the Court. In the absence of the Chief </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Justice</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Justice, </span><span>the Justice present who is senior in length of service shall preside. If it is otherwise impossible to determine seniority among the Justices, they shall determine it by lot and certify accordingly to the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> In addition to members of the Supreme Court there shall be other State Judges, who shall be citizens of the State and learned in the law. They shall include: (1) the Chancellor and the Vice-Chancellors; (2) The President Judge and the Judges of the Superior Court, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3 </span><span>of whom shall be Resident</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Associate</span><span> Judges and </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of whom shall after appointment reside in each county of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State; (3) the Chief Judge and the Judges of the Family Court; (4) the Chief Judge and Judges of the Court of Common Pleas, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of whom after appointment shall reside in each county of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State; and (5) the Chief Magistrate of the Justice of the Peace Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> There shall also be such number of additional Vice-Chancellors and Judges as may hereinafter be provided for by Act of the General Assembly. Each of such Vice-Chancellors and Judges shall be citizens of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State and learned in the law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> If it is otherwise impossible to determine seniority of service among the Vice-Chancellors or among the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Judges, they shall determine it by lot respectively and certify accordingly to the Governor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(e)</span><span> The tenure and status of the Justices of the Supreme Court and State Judges as shall have been appointed as provided for by the Constitution or by Act of the General Assembly </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">prior to the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">before May 14, 1951, </span><span>shall in no wise be affected.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 77. Amend § 3, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 3. Appointment of judges; terms of office; vacancies; political representation; confirmation of appointment.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 3. </span><span>The Chief Justice and Justices of the Supreme Court, the Chancellor and Vice-Chancellors of the Court of Chancery, the President Judge and Judges of the Superior Court, the Chief Judge and Judges of the Family Court, the Chief Judge and Judges of the Court of Common Pleas, and the Chief Magistrate of the Justice of the Peace Court shall be appointed by the Governor, by and with the consent of a majority of all the members elected to the Senate, for a term of 12 years each, and the persons so appointed shall enter upon the discharge of the duties of their respective offices upon taking the oath of office prescribed by this Constitution. The Governor shall submit</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> an</span><span> appointment within a period from 30 days before to 90 days after the occurrence of a vacancy howsoever caused. If a vacancy shall occur, by expiration of term or otherwise, at a time when the Senate shall not be in session, the Governor shall within a period from 30 days before to 90 days after the happening of any such vacancy convene the Senate for the purpose of confirming</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> an</span><span> appointment to fill</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> vacancy and the transaction of such other executive business as may come before it. Such vacancy shall be filled as aforesaid for the full term. Notwithstanding a vacancy, whether occurring when the Senate is or is not in session, an incumbent whose term has expired may hold over in office until the incumbent, or a new appointee, is confirmed and takes the oath of office for the next term, but in no event shall an incumbent whose term has expired hold over in office for more than 90 days after the expiration of the term. In all instances, the term of a new or reappointed Chief Justice or Justice of the Supreme Court, Chancellor or Vice-Chancellor of the Court of Chancery, President Judge or Judge of the Superior Court,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span> Chief Judge </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">and Judges</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">or Judge </span><span>of the Family Court,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span> Chief Judge </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">and Judges</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">or Judge </span><span>of the Court of Common Pleas, or Chief Magistrate of the Justice of the Peace Court shall begin after the occurrence of the vacancy and on the date the oath of office is taken, thus qualifying the individual to serve, but the appointment shall be forfeited if such oath is not taken within 30 days of confirmation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Appointments to the office of the State Judiciary shall at all times be subject to all of the following limitations:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">First, three of the five</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Three of the 5</span><span> Justices of the Supreme Court in office at the same time, shall be of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1</span><span> major political party, and </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two of said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2 of the </span><span>Justices shall be of the other major political party. </span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Second, at</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">At</span><span> any time when the total number of Judges of the Superior Court shall be an even</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> number</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> number,</span><span> not more than</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> one-half</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> half</span><span> of the members of all such offices shall be of the same political party; and at any time when the number of such offices shall be an odd number, then not more than a bare majority of the members of all such offices shall be of the same major political party, the remaining members of such offices shall be of the other major political party.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Third, at</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> At</span><span> any time when the total number of the offices of the Justices of the Supreme Court, the Judges of the Superior Court, the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Chancellor</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Chancellor,</span><span> and all the Vice-Chancellors shall be an even number, not more than</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> one-half</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> half</span><span> of the members of all such offices shall be of the same major political party; and at any time when the total number of such offices shall be an odd number, then not more than a bare majority of the members of all such offices shall be of the same major political party; the remaining members of the Courts above enumerated shall be of the other major political party. </span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(4)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Fourth, at</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> At</span><span> any time when the total number of Judges of the Family Court shall be an even number, not more than</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> one-half</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> half</span><span> of the Judges shall be of the same political party; and at any time when the total number of Judges shall be an odd number, then not more than a majority of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>Judge shall be of the same political party.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(5) </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Fifth, at</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> At</span><span> any time when the total number of Judges of the Court of Common Pleas shall be an even number, not more than</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> one-half</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> half</span><span> of the Judges shall be of the same political party; and at any time when the total number of Judges shall be an odd number, then not more than a majority of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>Judge shall be of the same political party.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(6) </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Sixth, before</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Before</span><span> sending the name of any person to the Senate for confirmation as the appointment of the Governor to a vacancy in any Judicial Office as aforesaid, the Governor shall, not less than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">ten (10)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">10</span><span> days before sending the name of such person to the Senate for confirmation, address a public letter to the President of the Senate</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> informing him or her that he or she intends</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> stating the intention</span><span> to submit to the Senate for confirmation as an appointment to such vacancy the name of the person</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Governor</span><span> intends to appoint.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 78. Amend § 4, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 4. Compensation of judges; method of payment; receipt of other</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> fees or</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">fees; </span><span>holding other office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 4.</span><span> The Justices of the Supreme Court, the Chancellor and the Vice-Chancellor or Vice-Chancellors, the President Judge and Judges of the Superior Court, the Chief Judge and Judges of the Family Court, the Chief Judge and Judges of the Court of Common</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Pleas</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Pleas,</span><span> and the Chief Magistrate of the Justice of the Peace Court shall respectively receive from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State for their services compensations which shall be fixed by law and paid monthly and they shall not receive any fees or perquisites in addition to their salaries for business done by them except as provided by law. They shall hold no other office of profit.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 79. Amend § 5, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 5. Composition of Superior Court; presiding judge; quorum.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a) </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 5. </span><span>The President Judge of the Superior Court and the Judges thereof shall compose the Superior Court, as hereinafter prescribed. In each of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> courts the President Judge when present shall preside, and in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the President Judge’s </span><span>absence the senior Judge present shall preside.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b) </span><span>One Judge shall constitute a quorum of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> courts, respectively, except in the Superior Court sitting to try cases of prosecution under Section 8 of Article V of this Constitution, when </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2</span><span> Judges shall constitute a quorum. One Judge may open and adjourn any of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> courts. </span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 80. Amend § 6, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 6. Sessions of Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 6. </span><span>Subject to the provisions of Section 5 of this</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Article</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Article,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2 </span><span>or more sessions of the Superior Court may at the same time be held in the same </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">County</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">county </span><span>or in different </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Counties.</span><span></span><span></span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">counties.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 81. Amend § 7, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 7. Jurisdiction of Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 7. </span><span>The Superior Court shall have jurisdiction of all causes of a civil nature, real,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> personal</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> personal,</span><span> and mixed, at common law and all the other jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the formerly existing Superior Court; and also shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the formerly existing Court of General Sessions of the Peace and Jail Delivery; and also shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the formerly existing Court of General Sessions; and also shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the formerly existing Court of Oyer and Terminer.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 82. Amend § 7A, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 7A. Jurisdiction of Family Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 7A. </span><span>The Family </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">court</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court</span><span> shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the Family Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 83. Amend § 7B, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 7B. Jurisdiction of Court of Common Pleas.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 7B. </span><span>The Court of Common Pleas shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the Court of Common Pleas.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 84. Amend § 8, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 8. Definitions of particular terms.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 8. The phrase "Supreme Court" as used in Section 4 of Article V of this Constitution and the</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">The </span><span>phrases "Superior Court," "Court of General Sessions of the Peace and Jail Delivery," "Court of Oyer and Terminer" and "Court of General Sessions" wherever found in the law of this State, elsewhere than in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">amended Article IV of this Constitution,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article,</span><span> shall be read as and taken to mean, and hereafter printed as, the Superior Court provided for in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">amended Article IV of this Constitution;</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article;</span><span> and the phrase "Chief Justice" wherever found in the law of this State existing </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on May 14, 1951, </span><span>elsewhere than in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">amended Article IV of this Constitution,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article, </span><span>shall be read as and taken to mean, and hereafter printed as President Judge of the Superior Court, as provided for in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">amended Article IV of this Constitution.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 85. Amend § 9, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 9. Jurisdiction of Orphans' Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">[</span><span>Repealed 75 Del. Laws, c. § </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">53</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">53.]</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 86. Amend § 10, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 10. Composition and jurisdiction of Court of Chancery; initiation and decisions in causes and proceedings.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 10.</span><span> The Chancellor and the Vice-Chancellor or Vice-Chancellors shall hold the Court of Chancery. One of them, respectively, shall sit alone in that court. This court shall have all the jurisdiction and powers vested by the laws of this State in the Court of Chancery. In any cause or matter in the Court of Chancery that is initiated by an application to a Judge of that Court, the application may be made directly to the Chancellor or a Vice-Chancellor. Causes or proceedings in the Court of Chancery shall be decided, and orders or decrees therein shall be made by the Chancellor or Vice-Chancellor who hears them, respectively.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 87. Amend § 11, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 11. Jurisdiction of Supreme Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 11. </span><span>The Supreme Court shall have jurisdiction as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(1)(a)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> To receive appeals from the Superior Court in civil causes and to determine finally all matters of appeal in the interlocutory or final judgments and other proceedings of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> Superior Court in civil</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> causes: Provided</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> causes; provided</span><span> that on appeal from a verdict of a jury, the findings of the jury, if supported by evidence, shall be conclusive.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(1)(b)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> To receive appeals from the Superior Court in criminal</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> causes,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> causes</span><span> upon application of the accused in all cases in which the sentence shall be death, imprisonment exceeding </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1</span><span> month, or fine exceeding </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">One Hundred Dollars,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">$100, </span><span>and in such other cases as shall be provided by law; and to determine finally all matters of appeal on the judgments and proceedings of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> Superior Court in criminal</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> causes: Provided,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> causes; provided,</span><span> however, that appeals from the Superior Court in cases of prosecution under Section 8 of Article V of this Constitution shall be governed by the provisions of that Section.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(1)(c)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> Notwithstanding any provisions of this Section to the contrary, to receive appeals from the Superior Court in criminal causes, upon application by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State in all causes in which the Superior Court, or any inferior court an appeal from which lies to the Superior Court, has granted an accused any of the following: a new trial or judgment of acquittal after a verdict, modification of a verdict, arrest of judgment, relief in any post-conviction proceeding or in any action collaterally attacking a criminal judgment, or a new punishment hearing in a capital case after the court has imposed a sentence of death, or any order or judgment declaring any</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> act</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Act</span><span> of the General Assembly, or any portion of any such</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> act,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Act,</span><span> to be unconstitutional under either the Constitution of the United States or the State of Delaware, inoperative or unenforceable, except that no appeal shall lie where otherwise prohibited by the double jeopardy clause of the Constitution of the United States or of this State. Notwithstanding anything in this Article to the contrary, the General Assembly may by statute implement the jurisdiction herein conferred.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(2)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Wherever in this Constitution reference is made to a writ of error or a proceeding in error to the Superior Court, such reference shall be construed as referring to the appeal provided for in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section (1)(a) and Section (1)(b) of this Article.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">paragraphs (a)(1) and (a)(2) of this Section.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(3)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> To receive appeals from the Superior Court in cases of prosecution under Section 8 of Article V of this Constitution and to determine finally all matters of appeal in such cases.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(4)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> To receive appeals from the Court of Chancery and to</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> determine finally</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> determine, finally,</span><span> all matters of appeal in the interlocutory or final decrees and other proceedings in chancery.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(5)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(e)</span><span> To issue writs of prohibition, quo warranto,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> certiorari</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> certiorari,</span><span> and mandamus to the Superior Court, and the Court of Chancery; or any of the Judges of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> courts and also to any inferior court or courts established or to be established by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> law</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> law,</span><span> and to any of the Judges thereof and to issue all orders,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> rules</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> rules,</span><span> and processes proper to give effect to the same. The General Assembly shall have power to provide by law in what manner the jurisdiction and power hereby conferred may be exercised in vacation and whether by </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>or more Justices of the Supreme Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(6)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(f)</span><span> To issue such temporary writs or orders in causes pending on appeal, or on writ of error, as may be necessary to protect the rights of parties and any Justice of the Supreme Court may exercise this power when the court is not in session.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(7)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(g)</span><span> To exercise such other jurisdiction by way of appeal, writ of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> error</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> error,</span><span> or of certiorari as the General Assembly may from time to time confer upon it.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(8)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(h)</span><span> To hear and determine questions of law certified to it by other Delaware courts, the Supreme Court of the United States, a Court of Appeals of the United States, a United States District Court, a United States Bankruptcy Court, the United States Securities and Exchange Commission, the highest appellate court of any other state, the highest appellate court of any foreign country, or any foreign governmental agency regulating the public issuance or trading of securities, where it appears to the Supreme Court that there are important and urgent reasons for an immediate determination of such questions by it. The Supreme Court may, by rules, define generally the conditions under which questions may be certified to it and prescribe methods of certification.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 88. Amend § 12, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 12. Composition of Supreme Court; designation of temporary Justices; quorum; opening and adjourning court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 12.</span><span> A quorum of the Supreme Court shall consist of not less than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3 </span><span>Justices. The entire Court shall sit in any criminal case in which the accused has been sentenced to death and in such other civil and criminal cases as the Court, by rule, or the General Assembly, upon the concurrence of two-thirds of all the members elected to each house, shall determine. In case of a lack of quorum by reason of vacancies in their number, incapacity, or disqualification to sit by reason of interest, or to constitute a </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three-member</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3-member </span><span>panel of the Court, the Chief Justice of the Supreme Court, or in case of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Chief Justice’s</span><span> absence from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, disqualification, incapacity, or if there be a vacancy in that office, the next qualified and available Justice, who by seniority is next in rank to the Chief Justice, shall have the power to designate judges from among the judges of the constitutional courts to sit in the Supreme Court temporarily to satisfy the number of Justices required by law. It shall be the duty of the judges of the constitutional courts so designated to sit accordingly. No judge shall be so designated to sit in the Supreme Court in any</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> cause</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> case</span><span> in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the judge</span><span> sat below. Any </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>of the Justices of the Supreme Court may open and adjourn court.</span><span></span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 89. Amend § 13, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 13. Administrative head of courts; supervisory powers; designation of judges to</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> sit in Court of Chancery, or the Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> sit.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 13. </span><span>The Chief Justice of the Supreme Court, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> absence from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, disqualification, incapacity, or if there be a vacancy in that office, </span><span>the next qualified and available </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Justice</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Justice, </span><span>who by seniority is next in rank to the Chief </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Justice</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Justice, </span><span>shall be administrative head of all the courts in</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the State,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this State</span><span> and shall have general administrative and supervisory powers over all the courts. Such powers shall include </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">but shall not be limited to</span><span> the following:</span><span></span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(1) Upon the approval of a majority of the Justices of the Supreme </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Court</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court,</span><span> to adopt rules for the administration of justice and the conduct of the business of any or all the courts in this</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> State: Provided,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">State; provided,</span><span> however, that any other of the courts in this State may from time to time, subject to the exercise of the power in this paragraph (1) conferred upon the Justices of the Supreme Court, adopt rules of pleading practice and procedure applicable to such Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(2) Upon written request made by the Chancellor, President Judge of the Superior Court, the Chief Judge of the Family Court, or the Chief Judge of the Court of Common Pleas, or in the event of an absence or incapacity, by the next qualified and available Vice-Chancellor, or Judge, who is senior in length of service, to designate </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>or more of the State </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Judges (including the Justices of the Supreme Court)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Judges, including the Justices of the Supreme Court, </span><span>to sit in the Court of Chancery, the Superior Court, the Family</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Court</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Court,</span><span> or the Court of Common Pleas, as the case may be, and to hear and decide such causes in such Court and for such period of time as shall be designated. It shall be the duty of the State Judge so designated to serve according to such designation as a Judge of the Court designated. The provisions of this paragraph </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> shall not be deemed to limit in any manner the powers conferred upon the judges of the Superior Court under Section 14 of this Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 90. Amend § 14, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 14. Power of law judges to grant restraining orders and preliminary injunctions.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 14. </span><span>The President Judge of the Superior Court or any Judge shall have power, in the absence of the Chancellor and all the Vice-Chancellors from the county where any suit in equity may be instituted or during the temporary disability of the Chancellor and all the Vice-Chancellors, to grant restraining orders, and the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> President Judge or any Judge shall have power, during the absence of the Chancellor and all the Vice-Chancellors from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her and</span><span> their</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> collective</span><span> temporary disability, to grant preliminary injunctions</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> pursuant to</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> under</span><span> the rules and practice of the Court of Chancery; provided that nothing herein contained shall be construed to confer general jurisdiction over the case.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 91. Amend § 15, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 15. Judges ad litem; limitation and expiration of commission; compensation; persons not disqualified.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 15. </span><span>The Governor shall have power to commission a judge or judges ad litem to sit in any cause in any of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> Courts when by reason of legal exception to the Judges authorized to sit therein, or for other cause, there are not a sufficient number of Judges available to hold such Court. The commission in such case shall confine the office to the cause and it shall expire on the determination of the cause. The judge so appointed shall receive reasonable compensation to be fixed by the General Assembly. A</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Member</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> member</span><span> of Congress, or any person holding or exercising an office under the United States, shall not be disqualified from being appointed a judge ad litem.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 92. Amend § 16, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 16. Scope of jurisdiction and process; costs.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 16. </span><span>The jurisdiction of each of the aforesaid courts shall be coextensive with</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> State. Process may be issued out of each court, in any county, into every county. No costs shall be awarded against any party to a cause by reason of the fact that suit is brought in a county other than that in which the defendant or defendants may reside at the time of bringing suit.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 93. Amend § 17, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 17. Jurisdictional changes by General Assembly; appeals to Supreme Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 17. </span><span>The General Assembly, notwithstanding anything contained in this Article, shall have power to repeal or alter any Act of the General Assembly giving jurisdiction to the former Court of Oyer and Terminer, the former Superior Court, the former Court of General Sessions of the Peace and Jail Delivery, the former Court of General Sessions, the Superior Court hereby established, the Family Court hereby established, the Court of Common Pleas hereby established or the Court of Chancery, in any matter, or giving any power to any of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> courts. The General Assembly shall also have power to confer upon the Superior Court, the Family Court, the Court of Common</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Pleas</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Pleas,</span><span> and the Court of Chancery jurisdiction and powers in addition to those hereinbefore mentioned. Until the General Assembly shall otherwise direct, there shall be an appeal to the Supreme Court in all cases in which there is an appeal, according to any Act of the General Assembly, to the former Court of Errors and Appeals or to the former Supreme Court of this State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 94. Amend § 18, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 18. Powers of Chancellor,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Vice-Chancellors</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Vice-Chancellors,</span><span> and Judges.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 18. </span><span>Until the General Assembly shall otherwise provide, the Chancellor and the Vice-Chancellor or Vice-Chancellors, respectively, shall exercise all the powers which any law of this State vests in the Chancellor, besides the general powers of the Court of Chancery, and the President Judge of the Superior Court and the Judges of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> Courts shall each singly exercise all the powers which any law of this State vests in the Judges singly of the former Superior Court, whether as members of the Court or otherwise.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Until the General Assembly shall otherwise provide, the Chief Judge of the Family Court and the Judges of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the Family </span><span>Court, respectively, shall each singly exercise all the powers which any law of this State vests in the Judges of Family Court, whether as members of the Court or otherwise, and the Chief Judge of the Court of Common Pleas and the Judges of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said Court,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the Court of Common Pleas,</span><span> respectively, shall each singly exercise all the powers which any law of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State vests in the Judges of the Court of Common Pleas, whether as members of the Court or otherwise.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 95. Amend § 19, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 19. Instructions to jury.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 19.</span><span> Judges shall not charge juries with respect to matters of fact, but may state the questions of fact in issue and declare the law. </span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 96. Amend § 20, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 20. Trial by court of issues of fact in civil causes.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 20.</span><span> In civil causes where matters of fact are at issue, if the parties agree, such matters of fact shall be tried by the court, and judgment rendered upon their decision thereon as upon a verdict by a jury.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 97. Amend § 21, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 21. Amendments in civil pleadings and proceedings by Superior Court; examination of witnesses and parties.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 21.</span><span> In civil causes, when pending, the Superior Court shall have the power, before judgment, of directing, upon such terms as it shall deem reasonable, amendments in pleadings and legal proceedings, so that by error in any of them, the determination of causes, according to their real merits, shall not be hindered; and also of directing the examination of witnesses and parties litigant.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 98. Amend § 22, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 22. Payment into court pending action for debt or damages; costs.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 22.</span><span> At any time pending an action for debt or damages, the defendant may bring into court a sum of money for discharging the same, together with the costs then </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">accrued</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">accrued,</span><span> and the plaintiff not accepting the same, if upon the final decision of the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> cause, he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> cause, the plaintiff</span><span> shall not recover a greater sum than that so paid into</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> court for him or her, he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> court, the plaintiff</span><span> shall not recover any costs accruing after such payment, except where the plaintiff is an executor or administrator.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 99. Amend § 23, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 23. Survival of action; executor or administrator as party; continuance.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 23. </span><span>By the death of any party, no suit in chancery or at law, where the cause of action survives, shall abate, but, until the General Assembly shall otherwise provide, suggestion of such death being entered of record, the executor or administrator of a deceased petitioner or plaintiff may prosecute the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> suit; and if a respondent or defendant dies, the executor or administrator being duly serviced with a scire facias </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">thirty (30)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">30</span><span> days before the return thereof shall be considered as a party to the suit, in the same manner as if</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she had voluntarily made himself or herself a party;</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> it had been done voluntarily;</span><span> and in any of those cases, the court shall pass a decree, or render judgment for or against executors or administrators as to right appertains. But where an executor or administrator of a deceased respondent or defendant becomes a party, the court upon motion shall grant such a continuance of the cause as to the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">judges</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">court </span><span>shall appear proper.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 100. Amend § 24, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 24. Security for stay of proceedings on appeal or writ of error.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
