<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 24. </span><span>Whenever a person, not being an executor or administrator, appeals or applies to the Supreme Court for a writ of error, such appeal or writ shall be no stay of proceedings in the court below unless the appellant or plaintiff in error shall give sufficient security to be approved by the court below or by a judge of the Supreme Court that the appellant or plaintiff in error shall prosecute respectively</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> appeal or writ to effect, and pay the condemnation money and all costs, or otherwise abide the decree in appeal or the judgment in error, if</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the appellant or plaintiff in error</span><span> fail to make</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> plea good.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 101. Amend § 25, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 25. Chief Register in Chancery: appointment; powers and duties.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 25. </span><span>The Court of Chancery shall appoint a Chief Register in Chancery to hold office at the pleasure of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">that</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the </span><span>Court. The Chief Register in Chancery shall be the Clerk of the Court of Chancery and shall appoint, with the concurrence of the Court, a Register in Chancery in each county who shall also serve at the pleasure of </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the </span><span>Court. The Chief Register in Chancery may also appoint other deputies, issue process, and enter judgment and do such other things as are according to law and the practice of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">court.</span><span></span><span></span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 102. Amend § 26, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 26. Prothonotary as Clerk of Superior Court; powers and duties; entry of testatum fieri facias.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 26. </span><span>The Superior Court shall appoint a Prothonotary in each county to hold office at the pleasure of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Court. The Prothonotary of each County shall be the Clerk of the Superior Court in and for the County in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she holds office.</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the office is held.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Prothonotary</span><span> may issue process, take recognizance of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> bail</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> bail,</span><span> and enter judgments, according to law and the practice of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">court.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court.</span><span> No judgment in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>county shall bind lands or tenements in another until a testatum fieri facias being issued shall be entered of record in the office of the Prothonotary of the County wherein the lands or tenements are situated. Such Prothonotary shall perform all duties heretofore performed by the Clerk of the Peace as Clerk of the former Court of General Sessions and the former Court of Oyer and Terminer. This </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section </span><span>shall not be interpreted to prevent the transfer of a judgment from any </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">court</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court</span><span> of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>county to the Superior Court of another county</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> pursuant to legislation</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> under a law</span><span> enacted by the General Assembly, nor shall it be construed to require the issuance or entry into the record of a testatum fieri facias when a judgment is transferred from a court of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>county to the Superior Court of another county</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> pursuant to legislation</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> under a law</span><span> enacted by the General Assembly.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 103. Amend § 27, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 27. Clerk of Supreme Court; term of office and compensation.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 27.</span><span> The Supreme Court shall have the power to appoint a Clerk to hold office at the pleasure of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Court. </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Clerk</span><span> shall receive from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State for</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> services a compensation which shall be fixed from time to time by the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> Court and paid monthly.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 104. Amend § 28, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 28. Criminal jurisdiction of inferior courts and justices of the peace; regulation of jurisdiction; indictment; jury trial; appeals.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a) </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 28. </span><span>The General Assembly may by law give to any inferior courts by it established or to be established, or to </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>or more justices of the peace, jurisdiction of the </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">following</span><span> criminal </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">matters following, that is to say –</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">matters: </span><span>assaults and batteries, carrying concealed a deadly weapon, disturbing meetings held for the purpose of religious worship, nuisances, and such other misdemeanors as the General Assembly may from time to time, with the concurrence of two-thirds of all the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Members</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">members</span><span> elected to each House, prescribe.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> The General Assembly may by law regulate this jurisdiction, and provide that the proceedings shall be with or without indictment by grand jury, or trial by petit jury, and may grant or deny the privilege of appeal to the Superior Court; provided, however, that there shall be an appeal to the Superior Court in all cases in which the sentence shall be imprisonment exceeding </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one (1)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1</span><span> month, or a fine exceeding </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">One Hundred Dollars ($100.00).</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">$100.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 105. Amend § 29, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 29. Justices of the Peace; </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">term of office; powers of the Chief Magistrate and Justices of the Peace.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">terms of office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 29. </span><span>There shall be </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">appointed, as hereinafter provided,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">appointed </span><span>such number of persons to the Office of Justice of the Peace as directed by law, who shall be commissioned as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(a)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> Upon first appointment and confirmation, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">a Justice of the Peace shall be commissioned for four (4) years:</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> for 4 years.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(b)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> Upon second or third appointment and confirmation, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">a Justice of the Peace shall be commissioned for six (6) years:</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> for 6 years.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(c)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> Upon fourth or subsequent appointments and confirmation, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">a Justice of the Peace shall be commissioned for eight (8)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">for 8 </span><span>years.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 106. Amend § 30, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 30. Justices of the Peace, Chief Magistrate, and judges of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">legislative</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">legislatively-created </span><span>courts; appointment by Governor; terms of office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 30. </span><span>Justices of the Peace and the judges of such courts as the General Assembly may establish, or shall have established </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">prior to the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">before May 14, 1951,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through underline"> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">pursuant to</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> under</span><span> the provisions of Section 1 or Section 28 of this Article, shall be appointed by the Governor, by and with the consent of a majority of all the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Members</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">members</span><span> elected to the Senate, for such terms as shall be fixed by this Constitution or by law. The Chief Magistrate shall be appointed and confirmed subject to the provisions of Section 3 of this Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 107. Amend § 31, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 31. Registers of Wills; depositions of witnesses; process; appeals to Court of Chancery; disqualification of Register for interest.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 31. </span><span>The Registers of Wills of the several counties shall respectively hold the Register's Court in each County. Upon the litigation of a cause the depositions of the witnesses examined shall be taken at large in writing and made part of the proceedings in the cause. This court may issue process throughout</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State. Appeals may be taken from a Register's Court to the Court of Chancery. In cases where a Register of Wills is interested in questions concerning the probate of wills, the granting of letters of administration, or executors' or administrators' accounts, the cognizance thereof shall belong to the Court of Chancery.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 108. Amend § 32, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 32. Adjustment and settlement of executors' and administrators' accounts; notice; hearing of exceptions in Court of Chancery; transfer of jurisdiction; appeals.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 32. </span><span>An executor or administrator shall file every account with the Register of Wills for the County, who shall, as soon as conveniently may be, carefully examine the particulars with the proofs thereof, in the presence of such executor or administrator, and shall adjust and settle the same according to the right of the matter and the law of the land; which account so settled shall remain in</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Register of Wills</span><span> office for inspection; and the executor, or administrator, shall within </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three (3)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3 </span><span>months after such settlement give notice in writing to all persons entitled to shares of the estate, or to their guardians, respectively, if residing within</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, that the account is lodged in the</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span> office for inspection.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Exceptions may be made by persons concerned to both sides of every such account, either denying the justice of the allowances made to the accountant or alleging further charges against</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her;</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the accountant;</span><span> and the exceptions shall be heard in the Court of Chancery for the County; and thereupon the account shall be adjusted and settled according to the right of the matter and the law of the land.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> The General Assembly shall have power to transfer to the Court of Chancery all or a part of the jurisdiction by this Constitution vested in the Register of Wills and to vest in the Court of Chancery all or a part of such jurisdiction and to provide for appeals from that Court exercising such jurisdiction.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 109. Amend § 33, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 33. Style of process and public acts; prosecutions in name of State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 33. </span><span>The style in all process and public acts shall be THE STATE OF DELAWARE. Prosecutions shall be carried on in the name of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 110. Amend § 34, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 34. Continuation in office and designation of certain judicial officers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 34. </span><span>The Chancellor, Chief</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Justice</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Justice,</span><span> and Judges in office </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on</span><span> and immediately before </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">May 14, 1951,</span><span> shall hold their respective offices until the expiration of their terms respectively and shall receive the compensation provided by law. They shall, however, be hereafter designated as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> The Chancellor shall continue to be designated as </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Chancellor;</span><span></span><span></span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Chancellor.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> The Chief Justice shall hereafter be designated as President Judge of the Superior </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Court;</span><span></span><span></span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Court.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(3)</span><span> The Judges shall hereafter be designated as Judges of the Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(4)</span><span> The</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Vice-Chancellor</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through underline"> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Vice-Chancellor,</span><span> in office </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on</span><span> and immediately before </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> May 14, 1951,</span><span> shall hold</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office until the expiration of the period of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">twelve</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">12</span><span> years from the date of the commission for the office of Vice-Chancellor held by </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">him or her at the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the Vice-Chancellor on May 14, 1951, </span><span>and shall receive the compensation provided by law.</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> He or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The Vice-Chancellor</span><span> shall continue to be designated as Vice-Chancellor.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 111. Amend § 34A, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 34A. Continuation in office and designation of judicial officers of the Family Court and the Court of Common Pleas.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 34A.</span><span> The Chief Judge and the Judges of the Family Court and the Chief Judge and the Judges of the Court of Common</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Pleas</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Pleas,</span><span> in office </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on</span><span> and immediately before </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> May 14, 1951,</span><span> shall hold their respective offices until the expiration of their terms, respectively, and shall receive the compensation provided by law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 112. Amend § 35, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 35. Proceedings pending at time of 1951 amendment; books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records,</span><span> and papers; effect of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">amended article</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1951 amendment</span><span> on Court of Chancery.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 35.</span><span> All writs of error and appeals and proceedings pending, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on May 14, 1951, </span><span>in the Supreme Court as heretofore constituted shall be proceeded within the Supreme Court hereby established, and all the books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records,</span><span> and papers of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Supreme Court as heretofore constituted shall be the books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records,</span><span> and papers of the Supreme Court hereby established.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> All suits, proceedings and matters pending, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on May 14, 1951,</span><span> in the Superior Court as heretofore constituted shall be proceeded within the Superior Court hereby established and all the books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records,</span><span> and papers of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Superior Court as heretofore constituted shall be the books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records, </span><span>and papers of the Superior Court hereby established.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> All indictments,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> proceedings</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> proceedings,</span><span> and matters of a criminal nature pending in the former Court of General Sessions and in the former Court of Oyer and Terminer, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">at the time this amended Article IV of this Constitution becomes effective,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">on May 14, 1951,</span><span> and all books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> records</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> records,</span><span> and papers of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> former Court of General Sessions and former Court of Oyer and Terminer shall be transferred to the Superior Court hereby established, and the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> indictments,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> proceedings</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> proceedings,</span><span> and matters pending shall be proceeded with to final judgment and determination in the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> Superior Court hereby established.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> The Court of Chancery is not affected by </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">this amended Article IV of this Constitution</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the amendment to this Constitution enacted by Chapter 109 of Volume 48 of the Laws of Delaware</span><span> otherwise than by the provisions with respect to a Vice-Chancellor or Vice-Chancellors.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 113. Amend § 36, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 36. Abolition of Orphans' Court; transfer of jurisdiction and powers.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">[</span><span>Repealed 75 Del. Laws, c. § </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">53.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">53.]</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 114. Amend § 37, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 37. Court on the Judiciary.</span><span></span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 37. </span><span>A Court on the Judiciary is hereby created consisting of the Chief Justice and the Justices of the Supreme Court, the Chancellor, the President Judge of the Superior Court, the Chief Judge of the Family Court, the Chief Judge of the Court of Common</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Pleas</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Pleas,</span><span> and the Chief Magistrate of the Justice of the Peace Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Any judicial officer appointed by the Governor may be censured or removed or retired by the Court on the Judiciary </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">as herein provided.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">under this Section.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> A judicial officer may be censured or removed by virtue of this</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section </span><span>for wilful misconduct in office, wilful and persistent failure to perform</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> duties, the commission after appointment of an offense involving moral turpitude, or other persistent misconduct in violation of the Canons of Judicial Ethics as adopted by the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Delaware</span><span> Supreme Court from time to time.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> A judicial officer may be retired by virtue of this</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section </span><span>for permanent mental or physical disability interfering with the proper performance of the duties of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(e)</span><span> No judicial officer shall be censured or removed or retired under this</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section </span><span>unless</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she has been</span><span> served with a written statement of the charges against</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the judicial officer,</span><span> or of the grounds of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> retirement, and shall have had an opportunity to be heard in accordance with due process of law. The affirmative concurrence of not less than two-thirds of the members of the Court on the Judiciary shall be necessary for the censure or removal or retirement of a judicial officer. The Court on the Judiciary shall be convened for appropriate action upon the order of the Chief Justice, or upon the order of any other </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3</span><span> members of the Court on the Judiciary. All hearings and other proceedings of the Court on the Judiciary shall be private, and all records except a final order of removal or retirement shall be confidential, unless the judicial officer involved shall otherwise request.</span><span></span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(f)</span><span> Upon an order of removal, the judicial officer shall thereby be removed from office, all</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> of his or her</span><span> authority, rights and privileges as a judicial officer shall cease from the date of the order, and a vacancy shall be deemed to exist as of that date. Upon an order of retirement, the judicial officer shall thereby be retired with such rights and privileges as may be provided by law for the disability retirement of a judicial officer, and a vacancy shall be deemed to exist as of the date of retirement.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(g)</span><span> In the absence or disqualification of a member of the Court on the Judiciary, the Chief Justice, or in</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Chief Justice’s</span><span> absence or disqualification the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Senior Associate Justice,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the next qualified and available Justice, who by seniority is next in rank to the Chief Justice,</span><span> shall appoint a substitute member pro tempore.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(h)</span><span> The Court on the Judiciary shall have:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(a)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(1)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The</span><span> power to summon witnesses to appear and testify under oath and to compel the production of books,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> papers</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> papers,</span><span> and</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> documents, and</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> documents.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">(b)</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">(2)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> The</span><span> power to adopt rules establishing procedures for the investigation and trial of a judicial officer </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">hereunder.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">under this Section.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 115. Amend § 38, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 38. Retired Judges and Justices; temporary assignment.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 38. </span><span>A former State Judge or a former Justice of the Supreme Court, who is retired and is receiving a state judicial pension and who assents to active judicial duty and who is not engaged in the practice of law, upon designation of the Chief Justice of the Supreme Court, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Chief Justice’s</span><span> absence from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State, disqualification, incapacity, or if there be a vacancy in that office, upon designation of the next qualified and available Justice, who by seniority is next in rank to the Chief Justice, shall be authorized to sit temporarily in the court from which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the former State Judge or Justice</span><span> retired or in any other court</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> to which to which he or she could be designated</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> designatable</span><span> under the Constitution and statutes of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State if </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">he or she</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the former State Judge or Justice</span><span> still held the judicial position from which </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">he or she</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the former State Judge or Justice </span><span>retired. Any person so designated shall receive compensation as the General Assembly shall provide. Nothing herein shall authorize the designation of any former State Judge or a former Justice of the Supreme Court to sit in the Supreme Court except temporarily to fill up the number of that Court to the required quorum. The term "State Judge" as used in this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section </span><span>means a Chancellor or Vice-Chancellor of the Chancery</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Court or a</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Court,</span><span> President</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> Judge</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Judge,</span><span> or Judge of the Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 116. Amend § 39, Article IV of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 39. Senior judges.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 39. </span><span>The office of Senior Judge is hereby created. Any retired judge of a court established by </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">this amended Article IV of this Constitution</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the amendment to this Constitution enacted by Chapter 109 of Volume 48 of the Laws of Delaware</span><span> or by</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> act</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> Act</span><span> of the General Assembly, who is duly qualified and appointed, may serve as a Senior Judge. The qualifications, manner of appointment, term of office, compensation, duties, and all other matters relating to the office of the Senior Judge shall be as specified by statute.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> Senior Judges are subject to the Code of Judicial Conduct and are subject to censure,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> removal</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> removal,</span><span> or retirement by the Court on the Judiciary in accordance with Section 37 of this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Article IV.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c)</span><span> Senior Judges shall not be counted for purposes of determining the political representation on any court or on any combination of courts under Section 3 of this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Article IV.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 117. Amend § 1, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 1. Time and manner of holding general election.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 1. </span><span>The general election shall be held biennially on the Tuesday next after the first Monday in the month of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">November,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">November,</span><span> and shall be by ballot; but the General Assembly may by law prescribe the means,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> methods</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> methods,</span><span> and instruments of voting so as best to secure secrecy and the independence of the voter, preserve the freedom and purity of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">elections</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">elections,</span><span> and prevent fraud,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> corruption</span><span style="text-decoration: underline">, corruption,</span><span> and intimidation thereat.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 118. Amend § 2, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 2. Qualifications for voting; members of the Armed Services of the United States stationed within State; persons disqualified; forfeiture of right.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 2.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(a) Every citizen of this State of the age of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> twenty-one</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> 21</span><span> years who shall have been a resident thereof </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1</span><span> year next preceding an election, and for the last </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">three</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">3</span><span> months a resident of the county, and for the last </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">thirty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">30</span><span> days a resident of the hundred or election district in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> may offer to vote, and in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> shall have been duly registered as hereinafter provided for, shall be entitled to vote at such election in the hundred or election district of which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> shall at the time be a resident, and in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> shall be registered, for all officers that now are or hereafter may be elected by the people and upon all questions which may be submitted to the vote of the people; provided, however, that no person who shall attain the age of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> twenty-one</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> 21</span><span> years after the first day of January in the year of our Lord, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">nineteen hundred,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1900, </span><span>or after that date shall become a citizen of the United States, shall have the right to vote unless</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person</span><span> shall be able to read this Constitution in the English language and write</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person’s</span><span> name; but these requirements shall not apply to any person who by reason of physical disability shall be unable to comply therewith; and provided also, that no person in the military, naval, or marine service of the United States shall be considered as acquiring a residence in this State, by being stationed in any garrison, barrack, or military or naval place or station within this State; and no person adjudged mentally incompetent or person convicted of a crime deemed by law felony, or incapacitated under the provisions of this Constitution from voting, shall enjoy the right of an elector; and the General Assembly may impose the forfeiture of the right of suffrage as a punishment for crime.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(b) Any person who is disqualified as a voter because of a conviction of a crime deemed by law a felony shall have such disqualification removed upon being pardoned, or after the expiration of the sentence, whichever may first occur. The term "sentence" as used in this Section shall include all periods of modification of a sentence, such as, but not limited to, probation,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> parole</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> parole,</span><span> and suspension. The provision of this subsection shall not apply to (1) those persons who were convicted of any felony of murder or manslaughter, (except vehicular homicide); or (2) those persons who were convicted of any felony constituting an offense against public administration involving bribery or improper influence or abuse of office, or any like offense under the laws of any state or local jurisdiction, or of the United States, or of the District of Columbia; or (3) those persons who were convicted of any felony constituting a sexual offense, or any like offense under the laws of any state or local jurisdiction or of the United States or of the District of Columbia.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 119. Amend § 2A, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 2A. Residence requirements in case of intrastate removal; election of President and Vice-President of United States; qualifications.</span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 2A. </span><span>The General Assembly shall extend to </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">any elector in the state</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">any elector of this State </span><span>who shall have changed</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> residence from </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>county, hundred, or election district to another, but who has not resided therein for a sufficient time so as to be eligible to vote in the county, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">hundred</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">hundred, </span><span>or election district to which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the elector</span><span> has removed, the right to vote for the choice of electors for President and Vice-President of the United States, but for no other offices, provided </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">such citizen</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the elector </span><span>would have been eligible to vote within this State had</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the elector</span><span> not moved, and provided that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the elector</span><span> is not entitled to vote for the choice of electors for President or Vice-President of the United States in any other </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">place</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">place, </span><span>and provided further that </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">such citizen</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the elector</span><span> would be an otherwise qualified voter under this Constitution except that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the elector</span><span> has not resided in</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> county, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">hundred</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">hundred, </span><span>or election district for a sufficient length of time.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 120. Amend § 2B, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 2B. Residence requirements of persons from other states; election of President and Vice-President of United States; qualifications.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 2B. </span><span>The General Assembly shall extend to a citizen of the United States who has resided in this State for at least 3 months next preceding an </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">election</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">election,</span><span> but who does not meet the residence requirements established in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Article V, Section 2 of this Constitution,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section 2 of this Article, </span><span>the right to vote for the choice of electors for President and Vice-President of the United States, but for no other offices, provided such citizen was either a qualified voter in another state immediately </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">prior to his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> before the citizen’s</span><span> removal to this State, or would have been eligible to vote in such other state had</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> remained there until such election, and provided that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> is not entitled to vote for the choice of electors for President or Vice-President of the United States in any other state and provided further that such citizen would be an otherwise qualified voter under this Constitution except that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the citizen</span><span> had not resided in this State for </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">1 </span><span>year.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 121. Amend § 3, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 3. Influencing voter; loss of vote; challenge; oath and affirmation; perjury.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 3. </span><span>No person who shall receive or accept, or offer to receive or accept, or shall pay, transfer, or deliver, or offer or promise to pay, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">transfer</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">transfer,</span><span> or deliver, or shall contribute, or offer or promise to contribute to another, to be paid or used, any money or other valuable thing as a compensation,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> inducement</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> inducement,</span><span> or reward for the registering or abstaining from registering of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">any one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">anyone</span><span> qualified to register, or for the giving or withholding, or in any manner influencing the giving or withholding, a vote at any </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">general or special</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">general, special, </span><span>or municipal election in this State, shall vote at such election; and upon challenge for any of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> said</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the</span><span> causes the person so challenged before the officers authorized for that purpose shall receive </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the person’s</span><span> vote, shall swear or affirm before such officers that</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person</span><span> has not received or accepted, or offered to receive or accept, or paid, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">transferred</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">transferred,</span><span> or delivered, or offered or promised to pay, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">transfer</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">transfer, </span><span>or deliver, or contributed, or offered or promised to contribute to another, to be paid or used, any money or other valuable thing as a compensation,</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> inducement</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> inducement,</span><span> or reward for the registering or abstaining from registering of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">any one</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">anyone </span><span>qualified to register, or for the giving or withholding, or in any manner influencing the giving or withholding, a vote at such election.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Such</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">The </span><span>oath or affirmation </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">under subsection (a) of this Section </span><span>shall be conclusive evidence to the election officers of the truth of such oath or affirmation; but if any such oath or affirmation shall be false, the person making the same shall be guilty of perjury, and no conviction thereof shall bar any prosecution under Section 8 of this Article.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 122. Amend § 4, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 4. Registration of voters; days for registration; application to strike name from list; appeals; registration as prerequisite for voting.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(a)</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 4.</span><span> The General Assembly shall enact uniform laws for the registration of voters in this State entitled to vote under this Article, which registration shall be conclusive evidence to the election officers of the right of every person so registered to vote at any </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">General Election</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">general election </span><span>while</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the person’s</span><span> name shall remain on the list of registered voters, and who is not at the time disqualified under the provisions of Section 3 of this Article; and no person shall vote at such </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">General Election</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">general election </span><span>whose name does not at that time appear in </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> list of registered voters.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(b)</span><span> There shall be at least </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">two</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">2</span><span> registration days in a period commencing not more than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">one hundred and twenty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">120 </span><span>days, nor less than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">sixty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">60</span><span> days before, and ending not more than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">twenty</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">20</span><span> days, nor less than </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">ten</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">10</span><span> days before, each </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">General Election,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">general election, </span><span>on which registration days persons whose names are not on the list of registered voters established by law for </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">such</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the general </span><span>election, may apply for registration, and on which registration days applications may be made to strike from the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> registration list names of persons on </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> list who are not eligible to vote at </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">such</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the general</span><span> election; provided, however, that </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">such</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the</span><span> registration may be corrected as hereinafter provided at any time </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">prior to</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">before</span><span> the day of holding the election.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(c) </span><span>From the decision of the registration officers granting or refusing registration, or striking or refusing to strike a name or names from the registration list, any person interested, or any registration officer, may appeal to the resident </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Associate</span><span> Judge of the County, or in case of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> disability or absence from the County, to any Judge entitled to sit in the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Supreme</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Superior </span><span>Court, whose determination shall be final; and</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the Judge</span><span> shall have power to order any name improperly omitted from the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> registry to be placed thereon, and any name improperly appearing on the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the </span><span>registry to be stricken therefrom, and any name appearing on the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> registry, in any manner incorrect, to be corrected, and to make and enforce all necessary orders in the premises for the correction of the </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">said</span><span> registry. Registration shall be a prerequisite for voting only at general elections, at which Representatives to the General Assembly shall be chosen, unless the General Assembly shall otherwise provide by law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: underline">(d)</span><span> The existing laws in reference to the registration of voters, so far as consistent with the provisions of this Article, shall continue in force until the General Assembly shall otherwise provide.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 123. Amend § 4A, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 4A. General laws for absentee voting.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 4A. </span><span>The General Assembly shall enact general laws providing that any qualified elector of this State, duly registered, who shall be unable to appear to cast</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> a</span><span> ballot at any general election at the regular polling place of the election district in which</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> he or she</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the qualified elector</span><span> is registered, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">either</span><span> because of being in the public service of the United States or of this State, </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">or his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">because of being a </span><span>spouse or </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">dependents</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">dependent </span><span>when residing with or accompanying </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">him or her,</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the qualified elector who is in the public service of the United States or of this State, </span><span>because of the nature of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the qualified elector’s</span><span> business or occupation, because of </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the qualified elector’s </span><span>sickness or physical disability, because of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">the qualified elector’s </span><span>absence from the district while on vacation, or because of</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the tenets or teachings of his or her religion,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> the qualified elector’s religious tenets or teachings,</span><span> may cast a ballot at such general election to be counted in such election district.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 124. Amend § 4B, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 4B. Uniform laws for absentee registration.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 4B. </span><span>The General Assembly shall enact uniform laws for the registration of voters of this State entitled to vote under this Article who are temporarily absent therefrom and in the Armed Forces or Merchant Marine of the United States, or retainers or</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> his or her</span><span> spouse or dependents when residing with or accompanying</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> him or her,</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> a person who is serving in any such capacity,</span><span> or who are absent from</span><span style="text-decoration: line-through"> the</span><span style="text-decoration: underline"> this</span><span> State because of illness or injury received while serving in any such capacity, upon application in person or in writing.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 125. Amend § 5, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 5. Electors privileged from arrest; exceptions.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span style="text-decoration: line-through">Section 5. </span><span>Electors shall in all cases, except treason, felony, or breach of the peace, be privileged from arrest, during their attendance at elections, and in going to and returning from them.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>Section 126. Amend § 6, Article V of the Delaware Constitution by making deletions as shown by strike through and insertions as shown by underline as follows:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>§ 6. Voting machine recording tapes, voting machine certificate, and absentee ballots; delivery to Prothonotary; duties and composition of court; quorum.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(a) The presiding election officer of each election district, following the close of the polls on the day of the general election, shall deliver all of the following to the Department of Elections:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(1) The copy of each voting machine recording tape containing the signatures of the election officers present at the opening and closing of the polls from each voting machine assigned to the presiding election officer’s district.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(2) One copy of the voting machine certificate, made and certified by law.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(3) Any other document or item required by law to be provided to the Department of Elections.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(b) The Department of Elections shall deliver all of the following to the Prothonotary of the Superior Court:</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(1) The documents or items provided to the Department of Elections under subsection (a) of this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section.</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(2) The absentee ballots.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-left: 18pt; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(3) Any other document or item required by law to be provided to the Prothonotary of the Superior Court.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
<p style="margin-top: 0; margin-bottom: 0; text-indent: 36pt; text-align: justify; line-height: 18pt"><span>(c) At 10 o'clock a.m. on the second day after the election the Prothonotary of the Superior Court shall present the documents or items provided under subsection (b) of this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section</span><span> </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section</span><span> to the Superior Court for the appropriate county, and the Court shall, at the same time, convene for the performance of the duties imposed on the Court under this </span><span style="text-decoration: line-through">section. </span><span style="text-decoration: underline">Section.</span><span> After convening, the Court, with the aid of the officers and sworn assistants as the Court shall appoint, shall publicly ascertain the state of the election throughout the county, by determining the aggregate number of votes in each office given in the election districts of the county and for every person who received votes for each office.</span><span></span><span></span></p>
